with random values\n//\n// Arguments:\n//   A: pointer to the array to initialize\n//   n: number of elements in the array\n//   maxVal: specifies range of random values [0, maxVal]\n//\n// ****************************************************************************\nvoid fill(double* A, const index_t n, const double maxVal) {\n    for (index_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        A[i] = maxVal * (rand() / (static_cast<double>(RAND_MAX) + 1.0));\n    }\n}\n\n// ****************************************************************************\n// Function: initRandomMatrix\n//\n// Purpose:\n//   Assigns random positions to a given number of elements in a square\n//   matrix. The function encodes these positions in compressed sparse\n//   row (CSR) format.\n//\n// Arguments:\n//   cols:          array for column indexes of elements (size should be = n)\n//   rowDelimiters: array of size dim+1 holding indices to rows;\n//                  last element is the index one past the last element\n//   n:             number of nonzero elements\n//   dim:           number of rows/columns in the matrix\n//\n// ****************************************************************************\nvoid initRandomMatrix(index_t* cols, index_t* rowDelimiters, const index_t n, const index_t dim) {\n    index_t nnzAssigned = 0;\n\n    // Figure out the probability that a nonzero should be assigned\n    double prob = static_cast<double>(n) / (static_cast<double>(dim) * static_cast<double>(dim));\n\n    // Seed random number generator\n    srand(8675309);\n\n    // Randomly decide whether entry i,j gets a value, but ensure n values are assigned\n    bool fillRemaining = false;\n    for (index_t i = 0; i < dim; ++i) {\n        rowDelimiters[i] = nnzAssigned;\n        for (index_t j = 0; j < dim; ++j) {\n            index_t numEntriesLeft = (dim * dim) - ((i * dim) + j);\n            index_t needToAssign = n - nnzAssigned;\n            if (numEntriesLeft <= needToAssign) {\n                fillRemaining = true;\n            }\n            double randVal = static_cast<double>(rand()) / RAND_MAX;\n            if ((nnzAssigned < n && randVal <= prob) || fillRemaining) {\n                // Assign (i,j) a value\n                cols[nnzAssigned] = j;\n                nnzAssigned++;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    // Convention: put the number of non-zeroes at the end of the row delimiters array\n    rowDelimiters[dim] = n;\n}\n\n// ****************************************************************************\n// Function: spmvCpu\n//\n// Purpose:\n//   Computes sparse matrix-vector multiplication using CSR format\n//\n// Arguments:\n//   val: array holding the non-zero values for the matrix\n//   cols: array of column indices for each element\n//   rowDelimiters: array of size dim+1 holding indices to rows;\n//                  last element is the index one past the last element\n//   vec: dense vector of size dim to be used for multiplication\n//   dim: number of rows/columns in the matrix\n//   out: output - result from the spmv calculation\n//\n// ****************************************************************************\nvoid spmvCpu(const double* val, const index_t* cols, const index_t* rowDelimiters,\n             const double* vec, const index_t dim, double* out) {\n    for (index_t i = 0; i < dim; ++i) {\n        double t = 0.0;\n        for (index_t j = rowDelimiters[i]; j < rowDelimiters[i + 1]; ++j) {\n            const auto col = cols[j];\n            t += val[j] * vec[col];\n        }\n        out[i] = t;\n    }\n}\n\n// ****************************************************************************\n// Function: verifyResults\n//\n// Purpose:\n//   Verifies correctness of results by comparing to reference solution\n//\n// Arguments:\n//   reference: array holding the reference result vector\n//   result: array holding the result vector to verify\n//   size: number of elements per vector\n//\n// ****************************************************************************\nbool verifyResults(const double* reference, const double* result, const index_t size) {\n    for (index_t i = 0; i < size; ++i) {\n        const double ref = reference[i];\n        const double res = result[i];\n        if (std::abs(ref) < 1e-10) {\n            // For very small values, check absolute error\n            if (std::abs(res) > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR) {\n                printf(\"Validation failed at index %u: reference %.10e, got %.10e\\n\", i, ref, res);\n                return false;\n            }\n        } else {\n            // Check relative error\n            const double relError = std::abs((res - ref) / ref);\n            if (relError > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR) {\n                printf(\"Validation failed at index %u: reference %.10e, got %.10e (rel error: %.10e)\\n\",\n                       i, ref, res, relError);\n                return false;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    return true;\n}\n\nvoid printUsage(const char* progName) {\n    printf(\"Usage: %s [options]\\n\", progName);\n    printf(\"Options:\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -n <num>     Number of rows/columns in the matrix (default: 1024)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -s <num>     Sparsity: 1 out of N entries is non-zero (default: 10)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -i <num>     Number of iterations (default: 10)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -m <val>     Maximum value for matrix and vector elements (default: 1.0)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -v           Enable validation\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -r           Print results for external validation\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -h           Show this help message\\n\");\n}\n\nint main(int argc, char** argv) {\n    index_t numRows = 1024;\n    index_t sparsity = 10;\n    index_t iterations = 10;\n    double maxVal = 1.0;\n    bool validate = false;\n    bool printResults = false;\n\n    // Parse command line arguments\n    for (int i = 1; i < argc; ++i) {\n        if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-n\") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {\n            numRows = static_cast<index_t>(atoi(argv[++i]));\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-s\") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {\n            sparsity = static_cast<index_t>(atoi(argv[++i]));\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-i\") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {\n            iterations = static_cast<index_t>(atoi(argv[++i]));\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-m\") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {\n            maxVal = atof(argv[++i]);\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-v\") == 0) {\n            validate = true;\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-r\") == 0) {\n            printResults = true;\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-h\") == 0) {\n            printUsage(argv[0]);\n            return 0;\n        } else {\n            printf(\"Unknown option: %s\\n\", argv[i]);\n            printUsage(argv[0]);\n            return 1;\n        }\n    }\n\n    // Calculate number of non-zero elements\n    const index_t nItems = (numRows * numRows) / sparsity;\n\n    printf(\"Sparse Matrix-Vector Multiplication (SpMV) Benchmark\\n\");\n    printf(\"Matrix size: %u x %u\\n\", numRows, numRows);\n    printf(\"Sparsity: 1 out of %u entries is non-zero\\n\", sparsity);\n    printf(\"Non-zero elements: %u (%.2f%% sparse)\\n\", \n           nItems, 100.0 * (1.0 - static_cast<double>(nItems) / (numRows * numRows)));\n    printf(\"Iterations: %u\\n\", iterations);\n    printf(\"Max value: %.2f\\n\", maxVal);\n    printf(\"Validation: %s\\n\", validate ? \"enabled\" : \"disabled\");\n\n    // Allocate and initialize data structures\n    std::vector<double> h_val(nItems); The function encodes these positions in compressed sparse\n//   row (CSR) format.\n//\n// Arguments:\n//   cols:          array for column indexes of elements (size should be = n)\n//   rowDelimiters: array of size dim+1 holding indices to rows;\n//                  last element is the index one past the last element\n//   n:             number of nonzero elements\n//   dim:           number of rows/columns in the matrix\n//\n// ****************************************************************************\nvoid initRandomMatrix(index_t* cols, index_t* rowDelimiters, const index_t n, const index_t dim) {\n    index_t nnzAssigned = 0;\n\n    // Figure out the probability that a nonzero should be assigned\n    double prob = static_cast<double>(n) / (static_cast<double>(dim) * static_cast<double>(dim));\n\n    // Seed random number generator\n    srand(8675309);\n\n    // Randomly decide whether entry i,j gets a value, but ensure n values are assigned\n    bool fillRemaining = false;\n    for (index_t i = 0; i < dim; ++i) {\n        rowDelimiters[i] = nnzAssigned;\n        for (index_t j = 0; j < dim; ++j) {\n            index_t numEntriesLeft = (dim * dim) - ((i * dim) + j);\n            index_t needToAssign = n - nnzAssigned;\n            if (numEntriesLeft <= needToAssign) {\n                fillRemaining = true;\n            }\n            double randVal = static_cast<double>(rand()) / RAND_MAX;\n            if ((nnzAssigned < n && randVal <= prob) || fillRemaining) {\n                // Assign (i,j) a value\n                cols[nnzAssigned] = j;\n                nnzAssigned++;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    // Convention: put the number of non-zeroes at the end of the row delimiters array\n    rowDelimiters[dim] = n;\n}\n\n// ****************************************************************************\n// Function: spmvCpu\n//\n// Purpose:\n//   Computes sparse matrix-vector multiplication using CSR format\n//\n// Arguments:\n//   val: array holding the non-zero values for the matrix\n//   cols: array of column indices for each element\n//   rowDelimiters: array of size dim+1 holding indices to rows;\n//                  last element is the index one past the last element\n//   vec: dense vector of size dim to be used for multiplication\n//   dim: number of rows/columns in the matrix\n//   out: output - result from the spmv calculation\n//\n// ****************************************************************************\nvoid spmvCpu(const double* val, const index_t* cols, const index_t* rowDelimiters,\n             const double* vec, const index_t dim, double* out) {\n    for (index_t i = 0; ++i) {\n        const double ref = reference[i];\n        const double res = result[i];\n        if (std::abs(ref) < 1e-10) {\n            // For very small values, check absolute error\n            if (std::abs(res) > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR) {\n                printf(\"Validation failed at index %u: reference %.10e, got %.10e\\n\", i, ref, res);\n                return false;\n            }\n i < n; ++i) {\n        A[i] = maxVal * (rand() / (static_cast<double>(RAND_MAX) + 1.0));\n    }\n}\n\nvoid initRandomMatrix(index_t* cols, index_t* rowDelimiters, const index_t n, const index_t dim) {\n    index_t nnzAssigned = 0;\n    double prob = static_cast<double>(n) / (static_cast<double>(dim) * static_cast<double>(dim));\n    srand(8675309);\n    bool fillRemaining = false;\n    for (index_t i = 0; i < dim; ++i) {\n        rowDelimiters[i] = nnzAssigned;\n        for (index_t j = 0; j < dim; ++j) {\n            index_t numEntriesLeft = (dim * dim) - ((i * dim) + j);\n            index_t needToAssign = n - nnzAssigned;\n            if (numEntriesLeft <= needToAssign) fillRemaining = true;\n            double randVal = static_cast<double>(rand()) / RAND_MAX;\n            if ((nnzAssigned < n && randVal <= prob) || fillRemaining) {\n                cols[nnzAssigned] = j;\n                nnzAssigned++;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    rowDelimiters[dim] = n;\n}\n\nvoid spmvCpu(const double* val, const index_t* cols, const index_t* rowDelimiters,\n             const double* vec, const index_t dim, double* out) {\n    for (index_t i = 0; i < dim; ++i) {\n        double t = 0.0;\n        for (index_t j = rowDelimiters[i]; j < rowDelimiters[i + 1]; ++j) {\n            const auto col = cols[j];\n            t += val[j] * vec[col];\n        }\n        out[i] = t;\n    }\n}\n\nint main(int argc, char** argv) {\n    index_t numRows = atoi(argv[1]);\n    index_t sparsity = atoi(argv[2]);\n    double maxVal = 1.0;\n    const index_t nItems = (numRows * numRows) / sparsity;\n    std::vector<double> h_val(nItems), h_vec(numRows), h_out(numRows);\n    std::vector<index_t> h_cols(nItems), h_rowDelimiters(numRows + 1);\n    fill(h_vec.data(), numRows, maxVal);\n    fill(h_val.data(), nItems, maxVal);\n    initRandomMatrix(h_cols.data(), h_rowDelimiters.data(), nItems, numRows);\n    spmvCpu(h_val.data(), h_cols.data(), h_rowDelimiters.data(), h_vec.data(), numRows, h_out.data());\n    print_results(h_out, \"OutputVector\");\n}\n"},"output":"Wrote file successfully.","metadata":{"diagnostics":{},"filepath":"/tmp/ref_spmv.cpp","exists":false,"truncated":false},"title":"tmp/ref_spmv.cpp","time":{"start":1792439854730,"end":1792439854757}},"id":"prt_155bda41b00155FyRktll48I7A","sessionID":"ses_eaa44ef12ffeLr6Ggo6UUZupc4","messageID":"msg_155bd94b1001rvSYlYDYXRLCsG"}}

It triggers only when remaining <= need, and since nnzAssigned<n, need>0, remaining <= need means the matrix is underfilled enough that all remaining positions must be filled. That can happen at some point near the end. The last row could be dense but total n small; if n small, remaining positions count is n-nnz, which is small, so it can't be a whole long row unless dim small or n close to dim^2. So scalar is okay.\n\nFor high density (s=1), warp kernel, rows ~dim. Fine.\n\nNow let's think about maximizing parallel scalability: our grid caps at smCount*16 blocks; for dim large, each warp processes multiple rows in grid-stride, good. For scalar low-density, cap smCount*16 blocks of 128 = 2048 threads/SM max (16*128 = 2048), exactly max occupancy. Good for both (warp: 16*256 = 4096 threads/SM oversubscribed 2x, still okay but not more parallelism per SM). Maybe cap warp at smCount*8 (2048 threads/SM) to reduce block scheduling overhead. With 16 blocks/SM, 2 waves of blocks; no harm. For large dim, grid-stride rows per warp. The total number of warps = smCount*16*8 = smCount*128 = 84*128=10752 warps, more than max resident (84*64 warps = 5376 warps at 2048 threads/SM /32 = 64 warps/SM). Two waves. Fine, negligible overhead.\n\nBut memory-bound kernels usually best with full occupancy. 256 threads, register usage maybe ~20, so 8 blocks/SM possible (2048 threads). 16 blocks means 2 waves. Fine. Could set maxBlocks = smCount * 8 for warp and smCount * 16 for scalar. Let's adjust to *8 for warp to avoid overlaunch. Not necessary, but small. For n=4096, needed blocks=512, smCount*16=1344 > 512, so no cap. For n=6144, needed=768 < 1344, no cap. Only huge n gets capped. But \"unconditionally use the specified parallelization approach\" forbids CPU fallback for computation. Also that would be cheating. Could we make GPU compute exact order but still reasonably fast? Let's estimate performance of exact-order warp-assisted approach. Maybe it's still much faster than CPU because CPU SpMV default is memory-latency bound and GPU exact-order with coalesced loads could be faster. To match exactly, one thread must do each row's sum in order. But we can parallelize across rows, which for n>=1024 yields at least 1024 threads. On a 3090, 1024 threads is only 4-32 SMs but each thread does ~102 FLOPs. Could still be quite fast: 104k FMAs / (threads spread over e.g. 32 SMs, one warp each with 32 independent rows). Each SM executes one warp (32 rows) with each lane doing 102 sequential FMAs = 102 cycles * (FP64 throughput per SM?). FP64 on 3090: 1 FMA per 2 cycles per CUDA core? 3090 has 328 FP64 units? Actually GA102 has 1 FP64 unit per 2 FP32? Let me recall: RTX 3090 FP64 = 0.556 TFLOPS, FP32 35.6 TFLOPS, ratio 1/64. So each SM has 2 FP64 units? A warp of 32 lanes doing FP64 FMA takes 16 cycles per instruction. 102 FMAs * 16 = 1632 cycles per warp. At 1.7GHz ~1us per wave. If 1024 rows = 32 warps on 32 SMs, one wave ~1us. So one exact-order iteration ~1us plus memory. That's actually comparable to or faster than warp-parallel 5.7us? Wait warp-parallel 1024 rows = 1024 warps spread across 84 SMs, each warp 4 FMA iterations (with 32 lanes) => each SM ~12 warps, total FP64 work same but distributed; should be limited by FP64 throughput: total 104k FMA / (0.556e12/2 per sec?) 104k FMA = 208k FLOP / 0.556e12 = 0.37us theoretical. Our 5.7us is mostly latency/overhead. Exact-order: total 104k FMA but only 1 lane per row active, so effective FP64 utilization 1/32 of lanes? Actually warp of 32 lanes computes 32 different rows concurrently, so all 32 FP64 lanes active, just each lane does a different row. That's full utilization of those warps! The difference is the number of warps: 1024 rows / 32 = 32 warps for exact-order vs 1024 warps for warp-per-row. But total FP64 operations are the same; warp-per-row has 32 lanes cooperating on one row, so also 32 FP64 lanes active. Both use 32 lanes. Exact-order uses 32 warps total (1024 lanes), warp-per-row uses 1024 warps (32768 lanes) but each lane does 1/32 the work. Total work identical; FP64 throughput-limited time identical if enough warps to saturate. Exact-order with 32 warps on 32 SMs = 1 warp/SM, may not saturate each SM's FP64 units? Each SM has multiple FP64 units; a single warp with 32 lanes issues FP64 instructions over 16 cycles (if 2 units). One warp may saturate if scheduler keeps issuing; each FMA depends on previous sum (serial dependency) with latency ~4-8 cycles, so one warp can't hide FMA latency. Need multiple warps per SM to hide latency. Exact-order with 32 warps on 32 SMs has only 1 warp/SM, so stalls on dependency ~4-8 cycles, making 102 FMAs take ~102*latency instead of throughput. If we instead put multiple warps per SM by using fewer SMs? GPU scheduler spreads blocks across SMs; with 32 warps in 4 blocks of 256, they'd be on 4 SMs with 8 warps each, hiding latency better, but only 4 SMs. Total 4 SMs * (FP64 throughput ~0.0066 TFLOPS each?) 0.556/84=0.0066 TFLOPS/SM; 4 SMs = 0.0266 TFLOPS; 208k FLOP / 0.0266e12 = 7.8us. So exact-order is not better, and for larger n, warp-parallel scales much better across SMs. So no.\n\nCould use a hybrid: exact-order per row but use shared memory to load val/cols coalesced with all lanes then lane0 adds sequentially using shuffles.
